# Runtime V2 Debt Ledger

This is the durable debt ledger for Runtime V2 work. Add new debt here when it
is discovered. Close debt only with evidence: commit, task, command, or linked
document.

## Rules

- Do not hide debt in `NOTES.md` only. `NOTES.md` is a handoff log; durable debt
  belongs here.
- Every debt item must have an owner: an epic, task, or explicit future
  decision point.
- Every runtime task must state whether it touches any open debt item.
- Closing debt requires evidence and a dated note.
- Raising a legacy LOC ceiling in `.loc-legacy-allowlist` is a debt decision and
  must update this file or the owning task evidence.

## Open Debt

**The Status column is authoritative, not this heading.** A closed row keeps its
place in this table rather than moving, so that the causal text explaining why
the debt existed stays where the next reader of that area will find it — the
same reason the Owner Overrides section below leaves historical owner cells
alone. As a result this table holds every row, and 38 of the ones in it are
already `Closed`. Scoping work by reading the section title, or by reading a row
without its Status cell, will therefore over-count what is outstanding; that
mistake has been made on this ledger more than once. Read the Status cell, and
when a row names an epic in its Owner cell, check that epic's own scope section
before treating the row as yours — a row can name an epic and still be excluded
by it.

| RV2-DEBT-027 | Rare Runtime V2 MT liveness flake: the second post-Task-2 `make runtime-v2-check` attempt failed in `TestMTChannelParkUnpark` with stderr `panic: async: double poll` from `task_polling_enter` (`runtime/native/rt_async_internal.h`). The retained artifact directory was `target/debug/.tests/TestMTChannelParkUnpark-2284652494`; focused rerun `SURGE_BACKEND=llvm SURGE_SKIP_TIMEOUT_TESTS=0 SURGE_MT_TIMEOUT_SCALE=3 go test ./internal/vm -run '^TestMTChannelParkUnpark$' -count=10 -parallel=1 -p=1 -v --timeout 120s` passed 10/10, so no stable repro or root cause is known. Precondition evidence (structural-cleanup epic, 2026-07-12): panic site `task_polling_enter` guard `rt_async_internal.h` ("async: double poll"); original failure environment WSL2 (`6.18.33.2-microsoft-standard-WSL2`, 32 CPUs), `SURGE_SHARDS` default, LLVM backend, in-suite parallel run; recurrence bound: `make runtime-v2-liveness-stress` (manual, quarantined — 50x park/unpark MT load + 3x TSan completion-pin suite) recorded green at the cleanup kickoff commit; the pre-remote-select epoch (2026-07-13) FAILED in the MT half and the race is now REPRODUCED: the 50x rerun hit `panic: async: double poll` once (run 6, died at 503ms vs ~4.6s normal), and the retained binary (`target/debug/TestMTChannelParkUnpark-729201643`, artifacts + `.sg` source in `target/debug/.tests/TestMTChannelParkUnpark-729201643/`) reproduces standalone at ~4% per run (3 failures in 83 iterations, `SURGE_THREADS=2 SURGE_TRACE_EXEC=1`, WSL2) — the first working reproducer since the original flake. The TSan half is clean (3x green same day). The panic aborts before the TRACE_EXEC snapshot flushes, so the trace is lost on failure; the fix owner should add a crash-path trace flush or an abort-time probe first. Poll-entry sites to reconcile: the worker turn (`rt_worker_turn.c`), the direct poll paths (`rt_async_poll.c`), and the inline ready-child poll (`poll_ready_child_inline`, `rt_async_task.c`) whose single-queue-reachability invariant is documented as needing re-derivation if a child is ever reachable from more than the popping worker's own local tail; the cleanup epic's structural moves (FC-1/FC-2) stay inside the channel-registry lock and do not touch `task_polling_enter/exit`, `mark_done`, or the worker turn (overlap verdicts in `15-tasks/01b-liveness-precondition.md`). ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED (2026-07-13, commit `8a31b9b5`): `ready_push_with_policy` (`rt_ready_queue.c`) checked status/enqueued UNLOCKED before taking the owner shard lock and pushed blindly after — a wake in that window enqueues the yielded task, a second worker pops it and stores RUNNING (pops and RUNNING stores serialize on the owner shard lock), and the blind push inserts a duplicate entry whose READY store overwrites RUNNING under the live poll; a third pop takes the duplicate and double-polls. Diagnosis method: the polling byte now carries the poll-entry SITE (`task_polling_enter(task, site)`), so the collision panic names holder+entrant+task snapshot — three probe repros all said worker-loop x worker-loop with status=RUNNING/enqueued=0, which eliminated the cross-shard, inline-child, and N=1-runner hypothesis classes and isolated the only path that stores READY outside the pop-serializing lock. Fix: the push re-validates the gate under the owner shard lock. Regression rows: `SP_READY_REQUEUE_BEFORE_LOCK` sync point + `TestRuntimeV2LifecycleReadyRequeueWakeRaceProof` (deterministic positive) and `...NegativeControl` (`-DRV2_DEBT_027_NEGATIVE_CONTROL` removes the re-validation and MUST double-poll — non-vacuity proven), wired into `runtime-v2-lifecycle-check`. Stochastic evidence: pre-fix ~4% per run (6 failures across epochs on the retained binary); post-fix 300/300 standalone runs green and a full `make runtime-v2-liveness-stress` epoch green (make exit 0 captured directly: MT half `ok 234.2s`, TSan half `ok 37.8s`, 2026-07-13). CLOSED. | Closed (fixed 2026-07-13) | Fixed pre-remote-select (commit `8a31b9b5`) | The double-poll race is reproduced with a deterministic sync-point/probe and fixed, or enough repeated full-gate/focused evidence proves the old row was a one-off unrelated to current runtime contracts. |

| RV2-DEBT-035 | Small-int inline representation (fixnum) eliminated the pervasive int/uint allocation leak: values within the inline range (int ±2^62, uint 0..2^63−1) never touch the heap, and `parse_uint_string` no longer leaks its per-digit scratch, so hot integer loops are allocation-balanced (`while i<1_000_000 { i = i+1 }` valgrind: 16,000,016 allocs = 16,000,016 frees, definitely lost 0). The residual is the heap-bignum TAIL: values outside the inline range stay boxed `SurgeBigInt`/`SurgeBigUint`, and because int/uint are `Copy` the drop system never frees them, so a genuinely-big int retained to scope exit leaks. Arithmetic that consumes a big intermediate demotes/frees it (`bi_finish`), so the tail only leaks values kept past their last consuming op. | Open | Fixnum RC phase (order-A step 2): reference-count heap bignums, atomic RC for MT-native | Heap bignums outside the inline range are reference-counted (or made droppable) and freed at last use / scope exit; a big-int loop shows definitely-lost 0. |
| RV2-DEBT-036 | Source int/uint literals carrying text lower to `rt_bigint_from_literal`/`rt_biguint_from_literal` (runtime base-10 string parse) re-materialized on every use, so a hot loop condition (`i < 1000000`) re-parses its constant each iteration. Now leak-free (parse scratch freed, result demoted to inline) but costs O(digits) transient alloc/free per use (~16 alloc+free/iter in the counter loop). MEASURED 2026-07-23 (evidence moved here from the withdrawn RV2-DEBT-071): the cost is two heap allocations PER DECIMAL DIGIT, every time the literal is evaluated. `parse_uint_string` (`runtime/native/rt_bignum_parse.c` ~76-101) walks the digits doing `bu_mul_small` then `bu_add_small`, each allocating a fresh `SurgeBigUint` and freeing the previous. In a loop this is per-iteration: `while i < 100000 { i = i + 1; }` costs 14 allocations per iteration — 12 for the 6-digit literal in the condition, 2 for the 1-digit literal in the body — giving `1,400,015 allocs` over 100,000 iterations. CONTROL: hoisting both literals into bindings takes the identical program to `17` allocations for the whole run, i.e. zero per iteration. Nothing leaks (allocs and frees balance exactly); this is pure churn. The fix is constant-folding an in-range literal to a tagged word at COMPILE time instead of parsing a decimal string at runtime. SEQUENCING NOTE from the RV2-DEBT-068 design review: this should land BEFORE any copy-on-write benchmarking, because it dominates the baseline — measuring reclamation cost against a baseline shaped by literal churn would make the numbers mean something other than what they appear to. CLOSED 2026-07-23. Fix: `emitConst` (`internal/backend/llvm/emit_term.go`) folds an in-range int/uint literal to a tagged fixnum WORD at compile time — `0 -> null`, otherwise `inttoptr (v<<1)|1` per rt_bignum_tag.h — with zero runtime materialization, replacing the per-use `rt_big*_from_literal` decimal parse. The text is the source of truth for the in-range test (`numlit.ParseInt64/ParseUint64`), because `IntValue` is 0 on int64 overflow; out-of-range literals keep the parse path unchanged. Measured: the 100,000-iteration counter loop drops from 1,400,015 allocations to 3 for the whole run (zero per iteration). Correctness verified across small/negative/zero/out-of-range/uint/arithmetic and at the exact fixnum boundaries (2^62-1 inline vs 2^62 heap; 2^63-1 inline uint vs 2^63 heap), LLVM matching the VM byte-for-byte on every case. Gate: `TestRuntimeV2InRangeLiteralsFoldToFixnum` asserts a hot literal-bearing loop's allocation traffic stays flat, with a negative control (reverting the fold reproduces the per-iteration churn). SIDE EFFECT worth recording as a method note: three heap-census tests had encoded this literal churn into their expected free/alloc counts — `TestRuntimeV2DropSelectSendArm` (select windows 2->0, 3->1), `TestRuntimeV2DropScopeExit` (scope-end 6->4, view-order 9->5), and `TestLLVMNativeBufferedChannelAllocatesSingleBlock` (buffered-vs-unbuffered delta +2->+0, revealing the whole historical difference was the `1:uint` re-parse and the ring buffer is genuinely co-allocated into the one rt_channel block). Each was recalibrated to the clean baseline, not silenced: the reductions were proven balanced churn (alloc fell with free; valgrind A/B on the completing scope-exit program shows byte-identical 1,320B/28blk pre and post, all from rt_heap_stats/rt_range, none from bi_alloc). This is exactly the review's Phase -1 warning — literal churn dominated these baselines, so folding it changes what every such census means. | Open | Closed (in-range literals fold to inline words at compile time) | Small literals that fit i64/u64 emit `rt_bigint_from_i64`/`rt_biguint_from_u64` (inline, zero-alloc), and/or loop-invariant literal materialization is hoisted; the counter loop reaches near-zero allocs/iter. |
| RV2-DEBT-037 | Integer range-for on the LLVM backend was broken independently of the int leak: `for i in a..=b` over ints lowered the `SurgeRange` value into the array-iterator path (`emit_iter.go` `emitIterNext` reads `{data@0,index@8,length@16}`, mismatching `SurgeRange {start,end,has_start,has_end,inclusive}` — it read `start` as a data pointer and dereferenced it). ROOT CAUSE: the numeric-range while-desugar (`normalizeNumericRangeFor`) never fired because `isNumericRangeFor` matched `ExprBinaryOp` but drop-emission wraps the loop-head range in `ExprOwnedTemp`, hiding the shape. STAGE A (DONE): `isNumericRangeFor`/`normalizeNumericRangeFor` unwrap `ExprOwnedTemp`, so integer `for i in a..=b` (and `..`, typed/untyped, uint) desugars to a while loop that works on both backends; gated by `TestRuntimeV2RangeForIntegerHead`. STAGE B (DONE): the LLVM iterator lowering (`emit_iter.go`) now emits a unified tagged iterator — a leading kind word (0=array, 1=range) selects the body, so `iter_next` dispatches a range cursor (steps start→end via the tagged int/uint runtime, honoring inclusive) versus an array cursor. A stored/passed/returned `Range<int>`/`Range<uint>` value now iterates correctly; gated by `TestRuntimeV2RangeForStoredValue` (differential-equivalent, arrays still pass). Residual tail: a fixed-width `Range<i32>` VALUE (non-ptr element) still falls through to the raw-range read (rare; the common WidthAny int/uint case is covered), and for-loop iteration on LLVM leaks per the shared protocol gap (RV2-DEBT-040). | Closed for int/uint (A + B landed); fixed-width range VALUES are a rare residual | — | Integer/uint range-for is correct on LLVM in both for-heads and stored values; gated by `TestRuntimeV2RangeFor{IntegerHead,StoredValue}`. |
| RV2-DEBT-040 | For-loop iteration via the generic iterator protocol leaks on the LLVM backend, independent of range-for: each `iter_next` boxes an `Option<T>` on the heap every iteration and the iterator struct (array cursor or range cursor) is allocated once, but drop-emission reclaims neither. Pre-existing and systemic: `for x in arr` over a 3-element array in a 2000-iteration outer loop leaks 10,000 blocks. CLOSED 2026-07-19 (Epic 20 Task 2, codex-implemented, lead-reviewed + independently valgrind-verified). ROOT CAUSE: `normalizeIterFor` desugars for-loops AFTER the sema pass that computes drop obligations, so the synthetic `iter`/`next` locals never acquire any — and a generic type-driven drop would have been WRONG anyway (the cursor is a hand-rolled 32-byte tagged blob whose declared HIR type is a type-checking fiction; a deep drop of the consumed Option box would double-free the moved-out payload). FIX: dedicated release channel — `StmtIterRelease`/`InstrIterRelease{Place, Cursor}`, synthesized in `normalizeIterFor`: the step box frees shallowly right after payload extraction (and on the exhausted branch) using its own concrete `Option<T>` layout; the cursor frees once after the while (covers exhaustion + break) with the FIXED 32/8 iterator layout, plus an injected release before every real `return` inside the body (recursive walker; `StmtRet` value-yields deliberately excluded). VM backend: no-ops (its iterator objects are ordinary GC'd heap values). ADVERSARIAL REVIEW ROUND closed two reopened leaks before commit: (a) a real `return` inside an expression-embedded block (compare-as-value arm, nested block expression) bypassed the injected cursor release — the walker now descends into every expression that can carry a return and discriminates real returns from implicit block-value yields (`IsImplicit`), which the review itself had conflated; (b) the cursor-release guard only trusted WidthAny int/uint Range elements and silently skipped every other kind — generalized to pointer-shaped-by-default (strings/structs/heap handles always release; only fixed-width numerics and bool match `emitRangeIterInit`'s no-alloc fast path and skip). The review's array-desugar diagnosis was DISPROVEN empirically (a `string[]` for-loop emits zero `__range` calls — arrays never classify as ranges), so the guard hole was real only for genuine Range<T> values. GUARDED RESIDUAL: a genuine Range<T> of fixed-width numeric/bool element keeps the historical leak (its fast path returns the SurgeRange pointer itself; the case is rejected at the surface today). Proof: valgrind "All heap blocks were freed" on 2000-iteration array (30,029/30,029), string[] (30,016/30,016), and expr-return (40,023/40,023) repros; `TestRuntimeV2IterProtocolReclamationCensusBalanced` (8 probes: bound var, break, return, expr-return, string[], struct[], stored range, d(2000)-d(1) differential; drop-reclamation gate). Found en route: RV2-DEBT-054 (while-body composite let leak), RV2-DEBT-055 (`for _ in` ICE), RV2-DEBT-056 (struct-element array boxes). | Closed (dedicated iter-release channel; loop-count-independent valgrind + census gate) | — | Done: `for` over arrays and WidthAny ranges is allocation-balanced on LLVM; the guarded fixed-width-range residual is recorded here and unreachable from surface syntax. |
| RV2-DEBT-054 | A boxed/composite `let` re-declared each pass through a `while` loop body leaks on the LLVM backend — no for-loop involved: `while outer < 2000 { let arr = [1,2,3]; ... }` leaks 2000 x 24B (the array header/backing of each pass; a stored `Range<T>` value leaks the same way). Found 2026-07-19 during the RV2-DEBT-040 fix (reproduced with the for-loop removed entirely; the iterator census keeps this confound out of its window by declaring iterables once, outside the measured loop). Distinct, general drop-emission gap: scope-exit obligations do not fire per-iteration for while-body locals (Epic 19's scope-exit synthesis covers function/block scope ends — the loop back-edge is not an emission point). | Open | Drop-emission follow-up (same family as Epic 19 scope-exit work) | A droppable `let` inside a while body is reclaimed on every iteration (back-edge or block-end emission); a census row pins d(N) independence; the RV2-DEBT-040 census rows can then drop their declare-outside workaround note. |
| RV2-DEBT-060 | Every `channel_on(...)` and every `.share()` leaks its caller-side `far Channel<T>` handle box: the LLVM backend allocates it via `rt_far_channel_handle_alloc` (emit_crossing_channel_create.go, emit_crossing_share.go) and no generated code path ever frees it — `rt_far_channel_release` is fully implemented in the runtime and NEVER CALLED from compiled code (zero references outside its defining file). Deterministic and shard-topology-independent: the strict-census valgrind program loses exactly 1,280B/52 blocks at 1, 2, and 8 shards. Root asymmetry: `far Task<T>` handles retire on `.await()` consumption, but `far Channel<T>` has no consuming operation — used repeatedly, never awaited away — so the handle box needs DROP GLUE, which channels do not have (the recorded pre-arc channel model, RV2-DEBT-048's residual class). A share() call additionally leaves the dispatch-side sibling lease still-reachable via the global registry (same pre-arc class, not newly found). Affects every Epic 16/17 e2e that creates channels; none ran under valgrind until the Task 8 strict census (2026-07-20), which asserts the exact documented bound at both tiers so any magnitude change trips. CLOSED 2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 7, FAR half only — see RV2-DEBT-048 for the local-channel residual this row's own text already flagged as a distinct class): the handle box now frees via a new `isFarChannelType` leaf case in `emitInstrDrop` (`internal/backend/llvm/emit_instr.go`, NOT `emitDropValue` — an earlier attempt to also add the case there, for composite-field drops, turned out to race a pre-existing bug and was reverted; see RV2-DEBT-061) dispatching to the already-implemented `rt_far_channel_handle_drop`. The owner-side `rt_channel` OBJECT (the RV2-DEBT-048 residual's far half) now frees too: a new `rt_channel_free` (`runtime/native/rt_async_channel.c`, single `rt_free` reconstructing the exact `rt_channel_new` allocation size from the stored capacity) is called from `release_entry` (`runtime/native/rt_far_channel.c`) — the single choke point every reclaim path (release, release_all's shutdown sweep, unpin-to-zero) already routes through, gated on the SAME `active_leases==0 && inflight==0` predicate `.share()`'s sibling leases already exercise correctly (Epic 16). Traced both mint call sites directly: the registry is the channel object's sole owner from creation on, so this is exactly-once by construction, not by convention. HONEST SCOPE: reclaims the channel object (struct+buffer) for any element type; does NOT drain buffered-but-unreceived payloads (raw bits, no destructor path — RV2-DEBT-060's non-copy half rides Epic 21's Task 8/fork 5). For `Channel<int>`/fixnum T — every program this fix's tests use, since ChannelCreate stays Copy-only until fork 5 — this is a complete fix, no payload can leak. New gate row `TestRuntimeV2DropFarChannelHandleAndObjectValgrindZero` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_drop_far_channel_e2e_test.go`): create + four independent `.share()` leases + scope exit, strict zero at 1/2/8 shards; deliberately does NOT exercise actual send/recv (`on ch {...}`) — see RV2-DEBT-061, a pre-existing, unrelated race that fuller send/recv programs hit intermittently and would make a census flaky. Negative control: 120B/5 blocks on unmodified HEAD (1 channel_on + 4 shares, matching `rt_far_task_handle`'s 24-byte size), 0/0 with the fix across 10/10 manual runs plus the automated gate. | Closed (handle box + owner-object free, FAR channels only; local channels are RV2-DEBT-048's residual) | — | A `far Channel<T>` binding's death releases its handle box exactly once — done; the strict-census tiers tighten from documented bounds to zero for the share/select verticals — done for create+share (this row's gate); sibling leases retire with their entries — done (registry predicate unchanged, now also gates the object free). |
| RV2-DEBT-059 | A task cancelled before its body ever ran leaks its compiled async frame: `spawn body(); child.cancel(); child.await()` — where `body` contains any internal `.await()` — leaks exactly one init state+payload pair per occurrence (32B direct + 24B indirect on the minimal local repro; the far-task caller-cancel shape carries the same defect plus its capture/handle state, 72B/occurrence), and leaks ZERO when the cancel line is removed. Root cause class: `rewriteAsyncReturns` deliberately keeps a cancelled frame's state alive (the runtime may re-park/re-enter it while scope children drain) and the pair is "abandoned once the task completes" — but no abandon-time drain ever reclaims it. Bounded and proportional to cancelled-task count, never growing per-task. Found 2026-07-20 by the Epic 20 Task 8 carried caller-cancel e2e (`TestRuntimeV2FarTaskCallerCancel`), whose valgrind census pins SAFE-AND-BOUNDED (crash-class valgrind errors fail; the loss must stay a clean multiple of the per-occurrence unit) instead of asserting zero, precisely so this row stays visible. CLOSED 2026-07-20 by `bdcc0695`: both cancelled exits (`rt_async_yield` and `rt_async_return_cancelled`) record the state and its TypeID-keyed drop function once on `rt_task`, and `mark_done` consumes and clears that obligation exactly once. The focused A/B behind `TestRuntimeV2CancelledSuspendStateReclaimed` removes the 32-byte outer state box (32B direct + 24B indirect before, 24B direct + 0 indirect after). The remaining nested `Task<T>`/`Channel<T>` handle leak is RV2-DEBT-062, not an unclosed part of 059; RV2-DEBT-061 remains an independently scoped crossing race, so the broader caller-cancel census is intentionally not misrepresented as strict zero. | Closed (outer abandoned async state reclaimed exactly once) | Epic 21 Task 6 (`bdcc0695`) | Commit `bdcc0695fd0e9074ad4a52d14bacb6eb77c6dc03`; `TestRuntimeV2CancelledSuspendStateReclaimed` plus the recorded pre/post Valgrind A/B prove the 059-owned outer state box is gone. Residual nested handles remain explicitly under RV2-DEBT-062. |
| RV2-DEBT-057 | Casting a union value between two structurally-identical but distinctly-interned union type IDs leaks the source box on the LLVM backend: `emitUnionCast` (internal/backend/llvm/emit_union_cast.go) allocates a fresh box and never frees the one it replaces — reproduces on a plain `let v: Outcome = Payload(...)` with no compare involved (16B definitely lost per cast, valgrind-confirmed on clean HEAD). Wide potential blast radius: any tag-constructor result assigned to a differently-interned union binding. Found 2026-07-19 during the RV2-DEBT-052 fix (its census probes construct scrutinees directly to avoid this confound; the residual constants in the tightened heap-capture census are candidates of this class). CLOSED 2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 1): `emitUnionCast` frees the old envelope exactly once after every payload field it needs is copied into SSA temporaries, guarded by the same `isRefType` ownership check the function already used for its deref branch (a borrowed `&T` source is never freed — only a genuinely owned source, which this cast always operates on: either a fresh tag-constructor temporary or a binding already consumed by the enclosing let/assignment's own move semantics). Verified: negative control (fix reverted) reproduces the exact pre-fix ledger shape on a 500-iteration loop (8,000B/500 blocks, valgrind); with the fix, that program and showcase 26 (a real, previously-shipped program using this exact `let out: State = Running(...)` pattern) both drop to 0 valgrind errors / a measured 108-byte, 9-block reduction respectively (showcase 26's small residual, 42B/2 blocks, is a separate, already-ledgered gap — RV2-DEBT-058's class — not this one). New gate row `TestRuntimeV2DropUnionCastReclamation` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_drop_union_cast_e2e_test.go`): an exact single-window free-count assertion (frees=1) plus an n=1/n=500 alloc/free differential proving the fix holds under a cast-heavy loop, not just once; wired into `runtime-v2-heap-check`. The heap-capture census residual constants (d=1, b=3) are NOT re-attributed by this fix — they persist unchanged, so they are not this class (falls to RV2-DEBT-058 or another cause; left for that investigation). | Closed (emitUnionCast frees its source envelope exactly once; guarded by the existing borrow check) | — | The union-cast source box frees exactly once — done; a census row windows a cast-heavy loop at strict zero — done (`TestRuntimeV2DropUnionCastReclamation`); the heap-capture census residual constants shrink or are re-attributed with evidence — NOT this fix, still open, re-attributed to investigate under RV2-DEBT-058 or a separate cause. |
| RV2-DEBT-058 | Compare-arm payload bindings never acquire scope-exit drop obligations: `Payload(s) => consume_locally(s)` type-checks via `inferComparePatternTypes` but the binding never reaches `registerDroppableBinding` (only the `ast.StmtLet` walk does that), so a heap payload bound-and-consumed-locally in an arm leaks — masked everywhere today because the ubiquitous shape `Success(x) => x` MOVES the binding out. Found 2026-07-19 during the RV2-DEBT-052 fix (its bound/guarded probes deliberately use the moving shape). Also the recorded reason RV2-DEBT-052's mixed-payload and whole-value-binding residuals had to err toward leaking: without binding-level obligations there is no safe owner to hand the box to. CLOSED 2026-07-30 together with RV2-DEBT-087, which is this row found again from the other side — the diagnosis here (only the `let` walk reaches registerDroppableBinding) was exactly right. See 087 for the fix and for the second half nobody predicted: the arm's drops were emitted before the arm's value, so an arm that READS its own payload freed it and then read it. | Closed (with RV2-DEBT-087) | Sema follow-up (teach the compare-arm binding path to register droppable bindings; then revisit the 052 residual arm shapes) | An arm-bound heap payload consumed locally reclaims at arm end; the 052 residual shapes (mixed payloads, whole-value binding) can then release through the binding instead of leaking; census rows pin both. |
| RV2-DEBT-056 | A 3-element array of struct values leaks its element boxes on the LLVM backend: 48 bytes / 3 blocks, CONSTANT regardless of how often the array is iterated or read (matches the struct-literal array construction, not any loop) — the array's own drop does not reclaim struct element boxes, or the literal's element temporaries never acquire obligations. Found 2026-07-19 during the RV2-DEBT-040 review round (the struct[] census probe cancels it in its differential). Epic 19's composite work covered array-of-droppables for strings/views — the struct-element case appears to sit outside that coverage. | Open | Drop-emission follow-up (verify against Epic 19's array-of-droppables rows, then fix or fold into RV2-DEBT-054's family) | An array of struct values reclaims element boxes exactly once when the array drops; a leaf census row pins it; the struct[] iterator probe can then assert strict zero instead of relying on differential cancellation. |
| RV2-DEBT-055 | `for _ in arr` (discarded loop variable) fails to COMPILE on both backends: "MIR validation failed: function main: local L3 (__iter1): unknown type" — `normalizeIterFor`'s element-type resolution (`data.VarType`, falling back to `ctx.bindingType(data.VarSym)`) never falls back to the iterable's own element type when the pattern binds no symbol. Pre-existing (verified on unmodified HEAD 2026-07-19, found during the RV2-DEBT-040 fix). Consequence: the discarded-payload branch of the iterator release (full drop of the unconsumed Option box) is correct but unreachable dead code until this is fixed. CLOSED 2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 1): added `iterableElementType` (`internal/hir/normalize_for.go`), a third fallback tried after `VarType`/`bindingType(VarSym)` both come up empty — it resolves the iterable expression's own type (peeling `&`/`own`/`*` wrappers), then tries `ArrayInfo` (dynamic arrays), `ArrayFixedInfo` (fixed-size arrays), and finally a single-type-argument generic struct instance (`Range<T>`, the only such iterable this protocol builds). Verified on both backends (LLVM and VM share the one HIR normalization pass): a negative control (fix reverted) reproduces the exact ledgered error text; with the fix, `for _ in <array>`, `for _ in <stored Range<T>>`, and `for _ in <fixed-size array>` all compile and run correctly. New probe `array_for_discard_n_times` added to the existing `TestRuntimeV2IterProtocolReclamationCensusBalanced` (`internal/vm/runtime_v2_iter_protocol_reclamation_e2e_test.go`, same n=1/n=2000 differential convention as its sibling probes) exercises the discarded-payload release branch this row's own text flagged as dead code — it is correct, no leak. | Closed (normalizeIterFor falls back to the iterable's own element type) | — | `for _ in <iterable>` compiles and runs on both backends — done; the discarded-payload release branch gets a census row — done (`array_for_discard_n_times`); the element type falls back to the iterable's element type when no binding exists — done. |
| RV2-DEBT-039 | `format(...)`/`fmt_arg(...)` with a STRING (or string-slice) argument double-frees on the LLVM backend (`format("x={}", fmt_arg("hi"))` → "double free or corruption"; `fmt_arg(s[0..50])` → "free(): invalid pointer"). An int argument (`fmt_arg(42)`) works. Independent of fixnum (the int path is clean) and of range-for; surfaced while fixing range-for (showcase 21's `result_str1[0..50]` head-slice). VM formats all argument types correctly. ROOT CAUSE: `FmtArg = FmtStr(&string) | …` held a BORROW; the type system collapses a `&string` tag payload to `string`, so drop-emission treats the borrowed handle as an owned string and frees a stack pointer (double-free). `&T` is a Copy type and must never be dropped — the collapse turned it non-Copy. FIXED: `FmtStr` now OWNS its string (`fmt_arg` clones once, `format` appends it in place via `append_fmt_arg` — copy count at parity with the borrow), which is the sound design (borrow-in-union is unsound here; see RV2-DEBT-041). Also hardened `emit_drop_glue.go typeOwnsHeapRec` to skip genuine `&T`/`*T` fields. Showcases 15/21/23 now match the VM; `format(...)` with string args is double-free-clean (valgrind). Residual: format's internal temporaries (slice ranges, concat intermediates) still leak per the general drop-emission gap (RV2-DEBT-040), not a double-free. | Closed (owned FmtArg) | — | `format`/`fmt_arg` with string/slice args run double-free-clean on LLVM, matching the VM (done). |
| RV2-DEBT-041 | Soundness gap: the borrow checker does not track a `&T` borrow stored in a union/struct that escapes its scope. `tag R(&string); fn make() -> Ref { let l = "x"; return R(&l); }` compiles with NO diagnostic — `&l` dangles after `make` returns; the VM catches it only with a runtime guard (`VM1001 used before initialization`) and LLVM silently UAFs. Lexical lifetimes + no full borrow-checker (LANGUAGE.md) mean borrows stored in data aren't lifetime-tracked. Surfaced while fixing RV2-DEBT-039 (which sidesteps it by making FmtArg own its data). CLOSED 2026-07-16 by disallowing the pattern (references are second-class values): sema rejects reference types stored in ANY user aggregate position — struct fields (`SEM3138`, decl + declared tuple/array type exprs in `resolveTypeExprWithScope`), tag payloads (`checkTag`), tuple/array/map LITERAL elements (`type_expr_values.go`) — and rejects returning a borrow that provably roots in frame-local storage (`SEM3139` in `checkBorrowEscapeOnReturn`: `return &local`, `let r = &l; return r`, `return &owned_param`; a `&T` param returned as `&T` stays legal via the existing call-result loan keep-alive). `@intrinsic` core types (BytesView) keep reference/pointer fields — the runtime pins them. `stdlib/json` was the one live user (param-derived refs in `Parser`/`ViewParser`) and was rewritten to own its data (byte[]/BytesView moved in). EXTENDED same day after an external (Codex) audit confirmed two more live escape routes: (3) `Channel<&T>` formed and `ch.send(&l)` shipped a borrow to a task that outlives it (LLVM died with an emit ICE, VM refcount-masked) — now `Channel<&T>` is rejected at type formation ("channel payload", covers `make_channel::<&T>` and annotations) and `checkChannelSendValue` rejects reference values outright (defense in depth); (4) returning a task handle whose spawn borrowed frame-locals (`return spawn worker(&l)`, incl. the laundered `let t = …; return t` form) let the caller await after the frame died — VERIFIED UAF (VM panics VM3301, LLVM silently reads freed memory) — now rejected (SEM3139 via TaskTracker spawn-args provenance); awaiting in-scope stays legal. Residual (documented, low-risk): a generic instantiation WRITTEN as a type (`Holder<&string>`) is accepted as an annotation but unconstructable (literals collapse `&T`→`T` and then mismatch); a ref returned out of a CALL result whose loan the table can't root (deep laundering through multiple frames) is not caught; a task handle escaping via ARG-PASS or container (not return) with borrowed locals is not provenance-checked — full escape analysis stays future work. Rebinding a ref local to an inner-scope borrow is blocked incidentally by the mutation guard (SEM3019) plus the no-default rule for uninitialized `&T` bindings. Policy helper: `internal/sema/reference_containment.go`. | Closed (pattern disallowed; kindness-first diagnostics) | — | Storing a borrow in an aggregate is a compile error at the declaration/literal (SEM3138, incl. channel payloads); returning a provably-local borrow or a local-borrowing task is a compile error (SEM3139); channel send of a reference is a compile error; goldens `ref_stored_in_*`, `ref_return_escapes_local`, `channel_payload_ref`, `task_borrows_local_escapes`, valid `ref_param_returned_as_ref`, `task_borrow_awaited_in_scope`. |
| RV2-DEBT-038 | Arbitrary-precision `float` (WidthAny) has the same never-freed heap shape the int leak had before fixnum: every bigfloat op allocates a `SurgeBigFloat` (mantissa biguint + struct) that the drop system never frees (`float` is `Copy`). SCOPED 2026-07-15: (1) Inline-as-f64 (the fixnum analog) is OUT — `float` keeps full arbitrary precision (`1.0/3.0` prints ~250 digits, matching the VM), so an f64 inline would change output and break the vm↔llvm differential; only exactly-f64-representable dyadics could inline (too narrow). (2) The parity-safe fix is to make WidthAny `float` a RECLAIMED type (freed on scope exit) while KEEPING value semantics (no move errors). That needs the ownership/borrow model extended to a heap-Copy scalar: a borrowed read (`rt_bigfloat_add(a,b)` — `const void*` params) must NOT clone, a stored duplicate (`let b = a`) MUST clone, every owner freed once — the SAME general mechanism DEBT-035 (heap int) needs. A naive "clone every copy" over-clones (leaks clones handed to borrowing ops); a naive "flip `float` to non-Copy" segfaults LLVM and hangs the VM even on `let a = 1.0; print(a)`, because move/drop/clone for a bigfloat exists in NEITHER backend yet. GROUNDWORK LANDED: `rt_bigfloat_clone`/`rt_bigfloat_free` runtime primitives + builtins registration. Remaining is a milestoned epic: [M1] native drop leaf (emitInstrDrop→rt_bigfloat_free)+typeOwnsHeap+move/clone; [M2] VM VKBigFloat drop/clone/move; [M3] sema auto-clone so value semantics survive (no move errors); [M4] flip IsCopy(WidthAny float)=false + async/channel/capture paths; verify no double-free/leak + differential at each. | Open (groundwork landed; milestoned epic) | Ownership-model reclamation for heap-Copy `float` (shared design with DEBT-035) | Bigfloat values are freed on scope exit; a bigfloat loop shows definitely-lost 0; vm↔llvm output unchanged; no double-free (valgrind). |
| RV2-DEBT-043 | Ergonomic over-rejection (SAFE — the checker rejects valid code, it never accepts invalid code): the borrow checker forbids a shared borrow of `x` in the same call that takes `&mut x`, e.g. `merge_sort(&mut a, &mut tmp, 0, len(a) to int)` → "cannot take shared borrow of 'a' while an exclusive borrow is active". The `&mut a` argument activates its exclusive borrow when type-checked and stays active across the sibling `len(a)` argument. It is actually safe: arguments are fully evaluated (so `len(a)`'s shared borrow ends) before the callee ever dereferences the `&mut`. Workaround: read the length into a local first (`let n = len(a) to int; merge_sort(&mut a, &mut tmp, 0, n)`) — applied to showcase 10_sort_merge. A correct fix is two-phase borrows: a mutable argument borrow is "reserved" during argument evaluation and only "activated" at the call, so a sibling borrow consumed to produce a VALUE arg (`len(a)`) does not conflict while a sibling borrow passed INTO the call (`&a`) still does. A naive "activate mut-arg borrows last" is UNSOUND (it would also accept `f(&mut a, &a)`), so this needs real analysis — a dedicated effort, not a sweep fix. CLOSED 2026-07-16 with two-phase argument borrows (design soundness-reviewed externally, incl. the explicit-arg question Rust sidesteps): a `&mut x` written DIRECTLY as a call argument (plain calls and method calls share the wiring) begins RESERVED — it lives in the place's shared list, so it blocks writes, moves, and other mutable borrows (a second `&mut a` argument rejects immediately, reservation-vs-reservation) while coexisting with shared reads like `len(a)`'s temp borrow. When the whole argument list is evaluated the reservation ACTIVATES into a normal exclusive borrow; activation rejects any borrow still alive at the call — an explicit `&a` sibling, or a loan kept alive through a ref-returning call (`ident(&a)`, generic or not). `&mut` nested deeper inside an argument keeps activate-immediately semantics (it can be used before the call). Method receivers were already de-facto two-phase (receiver borrows after args, live-arg check intact — `b.absorb(&b)` still rejects). Error-recovery hygiene: abandoned argument lists discard their reservations and eligibility. Dynamic indices stay conservative (index place segments are indistinguishable, so `f(&mut v[i], &v[j])` overlaps and rejects). Showcase 10_sort_merge restored to the natural `merge_sort(&mut a, &mut tmp, 0, len(a) to int)` and passes both backends. Verified over a 12-case matrix (goldens `two_phase_mut_arg_sibling_reads` / `two_phase_mut_arg_aliasing`); no existing golden shifted. OBSERVED ADJACENT GAPS (pre-existing, NOT two-phase, candidates for future rows): (1) `&mut`-param reborrow aliasing `fn f(a:&mut T){ g(a, a) }` passes both mut and shared reborrows of one place into a call with no diagnostic; (2) anonymous-fn captures are not loan-tracked at all (`take(&mut a, fn() -> int { return a[0]; })` compiles on clean HEAD too); (3) borrows held across `.await` live in the async resume frame — statically sound today because references cannot cross task boundaries, but the frame-storage question predates two-phase and covers every borrow kind. | Closed (two-phase argument borrows: reserve during args, activate at the call) | — | `f(&mut v, len(v))`, nested value-producing reads, and method forms compile; `f(&mut a, &a)`, `f(&mut a, &mut a)`, `f(&mut a, ident(&a))`, sibling moves/writes all still reject; showcase 10 uses the natural form. |
| RV2-DEBT-044 | Async awaitable-Task reclamation gap (leak, not a safety bug): every `await` leaks its awaitable. `checkpoint().await()` in a loop leaks ~88 bytes (48-byte `rt_task` + 40-byte inner) plus a small per-poll block PER ITERATION — `busy(1000)` awaited to completion loses 107,221 bytes / 3,070 blocks (~3/iter); the async/05_cancellation showcase loses megabytes because the busy loop runs tens of thousands of iterations before cancel. ROOT CAUSE: `lowerAwaitExpr` → `InstrAwait{Dst, Task}` → async-normalize `InstrPoll{Task, ReadyBB}`; the awaitable `rt_task` (spawned + refcounted by `checkpoint()`/`sleep()`, registered in the task table) is polled but never `task_release`d on the ready path (`ReadyBB`). Not cancel-specific — normal completion leaks the same per-await. Making the await's value operand consume=true is a VERIFIED NO-OP (async codegen regenerates the poll independent of it). FIX (async analog of RV2-DEBT-040): give `Task` a drop-leaf (`rt_task_release`/`free_task` — the cluster exists in rt_async_state.c), emit it at `ReadyBB` in async-normalize, and make the await CONSUME the awaitable so move-tracking prevents a NAMED handle (`let t = spawn f(); t.await()`, where `t` is also dropped by its scope) from double-releasing → UAF. Bounded (~4 spots) but real double-free risk; verify valgrind-clean + differential across all 8 async showcases. ROOT CAUSE CORRECTED ON FIX: the awaitable `rt_task` was NOT the leak — `rt_task_poll` already `task_release`s the target on the DONE path. The 48+40-byte pair per iteration was the async RESUME FRAME: the state struct box (`{__pc, __payload}`) plus the payload union box, allocated fresh at every suspend (`buildAsyncPendingBlocks` → `rt_async_yield`) and abandoned after the entry dispatch unpacked it (`__task_state` nulls `task->state`; `mark_done` also drops the final state on the floor). The small per-poll block was the discarded awaited `TaskResult` box: `drop` of a boxed union whose payloads own no heap was a backend no-op. FIXED: (1) `AsyncStateFreeBuiltin` (`__async_state_free`) emitted at the head of every pending block and before `async_return` (success only — a cancelled return's state can be re-parked by `apply_poll_outcome` while scope children drain, so its pair stays alive, one bounded pair per cancelled task); LLVM lowers it to shallow `rt_free` of both boxes + null-store, the VM treats it as a no-op (GC). (2) `emitInstrDrop` now frees non-heap-owning boxed unions through the same null-checked drop glue. Verified: `busy(1000)` awaited to completion 107,125 bytes / 3,068 blocks lost → 101 bytes / 5 blocks, iteration-independent (10 vs 5000 identical); async/05 megabytes → 16 bytes; zero invalid frees (named-handle double-await is rejected by sema — "task is neither awaited nor returned" — so no scope-drop double-release surface exists). Residual: a task cancelled mid-await leaks its final resume-frame pair + the parked awaitable `rt_task` (bounded per cancelled task, not per iteration). | Closed (resume-frame release + union-box drop) | — | `for`/`while` loops with `await` are allocation-balanced (valgrind definitely-lost independent of iteration count); named-handle awaits do not double-free. Done. |
| RV2-DEBT-042 | Soundness gap: coercing a borrow of a non-Copy type into an OWNED parameter emits an aliasing load + move on the LLVM backend, not a clone — so callee and caller both free the source (double-free). `fn f(x: string) {} … let s = "hi"; f(&s);` lowers `&s` to `Deref(Ref(s))` (a shallow handle copy), moves it into `f`'s owned `x` (freed at `f`'s exit), then the caller also frees `s`. The VM masks it by refcount-sharing. Surfaced via showcase 16 (`.find/.count/.replace(&needle)`); the STDLIB instances were fixed at the source by making those read-only string params `&string` (Option B — a borrow now binds directly, no clone, no move), but the underlying coercion remains unsound for any user code that passes `&owned` to an owned non-Copy param. Note: passing an owned VARIABLE (or literal) to a `&T` param already auto-borrows correctly; only the reverse (borrow → owned param) is broken. CLOSED 2026-07-16 (reject, kindness-first): at HEAD the plain-function and generic paths already rejected the coercion; the two LIVE holes were (1) METHOD calls — `typeKeyCandidates(&T)` exposes the bare `T` key for receiver lookup and `methodParamMatchesWithSubst` accepted it for owned params (`b.eat(&needle)` compiled and double-freed on LLVM) — now Copy-gated in `type_expr_calls_method_match.go`; and (2) STRUCT LITERAL fields — `ensureStructFieldType` compared `valueType`s, stripping `&` (`Box{ &l }` into an owned `string` field double-freed) — now rejected. Both report `SEM3137` ("takes ownership … only a borrow") with move/`.__clone()` hints via the shared `reportBorrowIntoOwned`; the plain-call path's old bare "expected string, got &string" upgrades to the same message. Copy-elem borrows (`&int` → `int`) still coerce fine. | Closed (rejected with SEM3137) | — | `b.eat(&needle)` and `Box{ &l }` against owned non-Copy targets are compile errors with move/clone hints; owned→`&T` auto-borrow, `&T`→`&T`, and Copy-elem deref coercions keep working; goldens `borrow_into_owned_method_param`, `borrow_into_owned_struct_field`, valid `owned_method_param_move_clone_ref`. |
| RV2-DEBT-045 | Directory-mode diagnostics are NONDETERMINISTIC for the type-identity mismatch class: `surge diag stdlib/` flaps between 4 and 8 errors across identical runs (even with `--jobs 1`), all of the shape "expected X, got X" (`field total_duration expects Duration, got Duration` in directives/benchmark+time, `expected &JsonValue, got &JsonValue` in http/context+response, `expected &string, got &&string` in fs). Two distinct TypeIDs for the same nominal type surface depending on module load order — a cross-module type-interning identity bug. Pre-existing (reproduces on clean HEAD with the pre-2026-07-16 binary); surfaced while differential-testing the reference-containment work; NOT caused by a stale installed stdlib (reproduced 2026-07-16 with a fresh install AND with SURGE_STDLIB pinned to the repo). CHARACTERIZED over 4 pinned runs: the identity-mismatch class splits into a STABLE subset (http/response.sg `&JsonValue vs &JsonValue` — 4/4 runs, a deterministic reproducer to debug first) and a FLAPPING subset (directives/benchmark+time `Duration vs Duration`, http/context — 2-3/4 runs, load-order dependent). Two unrelated stable bugs ride along and should be split out: fs/fs.sg:54 `expected &string, got &&string` (likely a plain fs.sg code bug — double-borrow at a call site) and the `saturating_cast` module importing a nonexistent `bounded` module (+ duplicate max_value/min_value declarations). Risks: flaky CI if stdlib diag is ever gated; user-hostile phantom errors in multi-module projects. CLOSED 2026-07-16. ROOT CAUSE (instrumented: Duration held TWO TypeIDs, 1508 and 1685, in one shared interner): one physical module compiled into TWO records because the walk keys it relative to the walk root ("time") while imports spell it from the repo root ("stdlib/time") — records were deduped by path STRING, so the module compiled twice and re-registered its exported types; consumers then bound the field type through one registration and a function signature through the other. FIX (driver): module records dedupe by PHYSICAL IDENTITY — directory for directory-shaped modules, file for file-shaped modules (sibling file-modules in one directory must not collapse; the first dir-only attempt aliased module_multitest's `import point` to main itself) — in BOTH graphs (`resolveDirModuleGraph`, `runModuleGraph`); every importer's import path is rewritten to the owning record and alias keys are threaded into `collectModuleExports` so alternate spellings see the exports AS the owner compiles (adding them after all compiles left consumers with "module has no exports"). Also (sema) the three `for key := range tc.exports` suffix-match fallbacks now pick deterministically (`moduleKeyByAliasSuffix`: exact key, then smallest suffix match) instead of relying on Go map order. Rode-along stdlib bugs fixed the same day: fs.sg double-borrow at a projection call site, and saturating_cast's dangling `import ./bounded` (commit f923bea4 had moved min_value/max_value into core intrinsics and deleted bounded.sg — the stale import caused both the missing-module AND duplicate-declaration errors; note saturating_cast has zero in-tree importers, a cull candidate for the post-v2 stdlib refresh). | Closed (dedupe by physical module identity + deterministic alias resolution) | — | `surge diag stdlib/` returns the IDENTICAL, empty error set across repeated runs with parallel jobs (verified 5/5 ×0 errors); goldens regenerate byte-identical; module_multitest sibling file-modules keep compiling. |
| RV2-DEBT-046 | async/02_fanout_fanin intermittently HANGS on the LLVM backend (futex-parked, all 66 threads sleeping, no ready task): ~1-in-20 standalone runs on the reference host, measured 2026-07-16 at 1/20 on clean HEAD (`a51940be`, pre-RV2-DEBT-044-fix binary) AND 1/20 with the DEBT-044 fix — PRE-EXISTING, rate unchanged by the resume-frame reclamation. Shape: 8 `spawn job(i)` into a `Task<int>[]` array, sequential `raw_task.await()` fan-in over `sleep((x*5):uint)` jobs; the hang parks forever instead of panicking "async deadlock", so it looks like a lost wake in the sleep-timer/join path under multi-worker scheduling (same family as the RV2-DEBT-002 lost-wake class, which was fixed for channel keys specifically). `scripts/run_showcases.sh` has NO per-run timeout, so one hung run stalls the whole differential suite indefinitely (interacts with RV2-DEBT-006's timeout-ownership debt). CLOSED 2026-07-16. ROOT CAUSE (gdb state dump of a caught hang: joiner task TASK_WAITING with park_key=JOIN(sleep-task), sleep task DONE, EVERY waiter store empty): the deferred stale-key removal in `wake_task_with_policy` is generation-qualified only for CHANNEL keys (the RV2-DEBT-002 fix); for a JOIN key the removal is UNQUALIFIED and `remove_waiter_from_store_seq(seq=0)` sweeps EVERY (key, task) match. A spurious wake consumes the joiner's park and defers the removal; the joiner re-polls and re-parks on the SAME join key in the post-unlock window; the removal then eats the fresh registration too. Join wakes are store-driven (mark_done -> wake_key_all pops the join store), so the joiner never wakes — parked forever, no deadlock panic. FIX: join keys are exempt from the deferred removal (`RT_DEBT046_STALE_KEY_REMOVABLE`, rt_sync_point.h); a stale join entry is self-cleaning — the target completes exactly once, its drain pops every entry for the key, and the stale pop is absorbed as one spurious wake by the wake token. Timer keys stay removable (their wake is by-id from the sleep deadline index, never store-driven); channel keys keep the generation-qualified removal. PROOF: deterministic sync-point harness (`SP_WAKE_BEFORE_STALE_REMOVAL` + `debt046-join-stale-removal-proof`, positive at SURGE_SHARDS=1/2/8, negative control `RV2_DEBT_046_NEGATIVE_CONTROL` strands with the expected message); empirically 0 hangs / 1000 consecutive standalone runs (baseline ~1 in 20-30). run_showcases.sh now bounds every run (`SHOWCASE_RUN_TIMEOUT`, default 120s; exit 124 fails the case with a TIMEOUT note instead of stalling the suite). Runtime C debug objects now build with -g (target/debug profile), which made the gdb state-dump diagnosis possible. | Closed (join keys exempt from deferred removal; sync-point proof + 1000-run gate) | — | Fan-in survives >=1000 consecutive runs (was >=200 required); `TestRuntimeV2LifecycleDebt046JoinStaleRemovalProof` + negative control gate the window; run_showcases.sh fails hung runs in bounded time. |
| RV2-DEBT-047 | The transport shutdown drain can PANIC on valid queued crossing messages: `rt_remote_spawn_fail_all_pending` (`runtime/native/rt_remote_spawn.c`) drains every shard's inbound queue at shutdown behind an allowlist that admits only `REMOTE_SPAWN_*`/`REMOTE_TASK_*`/`NONE` kinds — any queued `IMMEDIATE_ON_*`, `FAR_CHANNEL_*` (create/share/select request+reply), or `CREDIT_CONTROL` message hits `panic_msg("remote spawn: unsupported transport message kind during shutdown")` BEFORE the release helpers run, even though `rt_remote_task_release_msg_payload` (`runtime/native/rt_remote_task_completion.c`) already knows how to release exactly those payload kinds. The allowlist predates the immediate-on (Epic 13) and far-channel/select (Epics 14/17) message families and was never widened. Found 2026-07-17 during the Epic 20 draft review (codex second opinion, confirmed by direct code read). Reachability window: a message enqueued after the last steady-state drain and before shutdown drain; no observed in-suite failure yet — a deterministic repro row is part of the fix. CLOSED 2026-07-17 by Epic 20 Task 5 row 1 (commit `9f27d86d` + the harness row): the allowlist is now a kind-complete release switch (every production kind falls through to `remote_spawn_release_msg_payload` + `rt_remote_task_release_msg_payload`; only truly unknown kinds fail closed with "unknown transport message kind during shutdown"), and the switch itself is the static exhaustiveness guard (`-Wswitch` fails the build until a new kind takes a release stance). Proof: `shutdown-queued-kinds` harness mode (enqueues IMMEDIATE_ON execute/reply, FAR_CHANNEL create/share/select request+reply, CREDIT_CONTROL, then calls `rt_remote_spawn_fail_all_pending` — no panic, queues drained) + updated static-shape assertions pinning the new message and per-kind case coverage (`TestRuntimeV2RemotePublicationFailurePathStaticGuards`). | Closed (kind-complete release switch + queued-at-shutdown harness row) | — | Done: shutdown drain releases every production kind; the exhaustiveness guard is the release switch itself; the queued-message row covers the post-Epic-13 families. |
| RV2-DEBT-048 | SOUNDNESS: select send arms are invisible to ownership. Three linked gaps, verified 2026-07-17 on LLVM with Epic 19 drops active (probe corrected the same day: STRING LITERALS ARE STATIC and never heap-allocate, so the first literal-payload probe round was vacuous — all rows below use loop-built heap strings). (1) Marker inconsistency: direct `ch.send(s)` rejects with `SEM3015: expected own string` demanding `ch.send(own s)`, but the same spelling inside a select arm compiles bare. (2) The payload is never marked moved: using `s` after a select whose send arm WON compiles and runs (use-after-move read — printed the delivered value). (3) DOUBLE FREE, deterministic 3/3: when the send arm wins, the delivered value is freed by the receiver's scope exit AND by the sender's scope exit (`free(): double free detected in tcache 2`, native LLVM). A LOSING send arm reclaims correctly (payload freed once at sender scope exit; the residual definitely-lost blocks are the two `rt_channel_new` channel OBJECTS — runtime-backed Channel values have no drop glue, the known pre-arc model for runtime handles, out of the drop arc's current scope). No unsafe/@intrinsic involved. RESIDUAL UPDATE 2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 7): the residual's FAR half is closed — see RV2-DEBT-060, which gives the owner-side channel object a free path via the far-registry's existing lease-count reclaim predicate. The LOCAL half stays open and is now precisely bounded, not just deferred: `Channel<T>` is `@copy` (`core/intrinsics.sg`), and `isDroppableType` (`internal/sema/drop_obligations.go`) excludes Copy types before any other check, so a local `own Channel<T>` binding never acquires a scope-exit drop obligation at all — there is no leaf-gated-but-missing case to wire up, unlike the far handle. `struct rt_channel` carries no refcount field and `rt_channel_close` only sets a flag; making local channels droppable needs either refcounting added to a type kept Copy on purpose (the golden `let sender_ch = ch; let recv_ch = ch;` fan-out shape depends on it) or an affine redesign with its own `.share()` equivalent — a language-semantics decision, not a backend task. Epic 21 forks 4-5 (`21-owner-routed-frees.md`) already anticipated exactly this contingency and recorded the fallback of deferring it; no new debt row needed for the local half, this note is that deferral's record. Fix direction (Epic 20 design review, fork 6): send-arm payloads join move tracking with PER-BRANCH move semantics — moved in the winner branch, still owned in every other arm's branch, maybe-moved (unusable, kind diagnostic) after the join — which is exactly the Epic 19 per-arm drop synthesis pattern; require the `own` marker for spelling consistency. CLOSED 2026-07-17 by Epic 20 Task 3 (`20-tasks/03-select-send-arm-ownership.md`): `typeSelectAwaitExpr` now applies direct-send ownership discipline to local send arms (payload assignability vs the channel payload type; `own` marker for non-copy payloads — SEM3140; whole-binding payloads — SEM3141), and `typeSelectExpr` runs the compare-expression per-arm machinery (per-arm moved snapshots, union merge, `ArmDropsExpr` one-sided drop synthesis) so losing arms reclaim and post-join use is use-of-moved (SEM3130). The declared-outside-the-loop retry shape hits the standing back-edge conservatism with its recreate-each-iteration hint; the accepted retry form declares the payload inside the loop. Proof: goldens `select_send_missing_own`, `select_send_temp_payload`, `select_send_use_after_join`, `select_send_payload_type_mismatch`, `select_send_move_across_loop_backedge`, valid `select_send_own_binding` (losing-arm use + retry form); `TestRuntimeV2DropSelectSendArm` (exact free-count windows: winner-select 2 / winner-recv 0 / winner-drop-got 2 = payload+box exactly once at the receiver; loser-select 3 incl. in-arm reclaim; `SURGE_THREADS=1,2`) wired into `runtime-v2-heap-check`; VM parity via the VM sanitizer (same program clean on both backends); the pre-fix 3/3 native double-free reproducer runs clean; manual valgrind winner/loser 0 definitely-lost. `make check` + byte-stable golden regen + Sentrux `internal` 6484→6486 green. | Closed (per-branch move semantics + own marker; SEM3140/3141; heap-check rows) | — | Winning send arm frees the payload exactly once (census rows, loop-built payloads) — done; losing arms keep the value usable inside their bodies and reclaim at branch end — done; use after the select join is a compile error — done (SEM3130 + move hint); spelling matches direct send — done; accept+reject goldens — done. |
| RV2-DEBT-049 | An `@entrypoint` async main body can silently not execute (or stop mid-body) with process exit 0. Observed 2026-07-17 under valgrind on the select probes, BIMODAL across identical invocations: full runs allocate normally, truncated runs exit 0 with runtime-baseline-only heap traffic and missing program output. ESCALATED same day: reproduced NATIVELY (no valgrind) in bursts — during a bad window the same async-main binary fails 4-6 consecutive runs (exit 0, zero stdout), then passes 20/20 minutes later; host-state/timing dependent (WSL2, 32 CPUs, low load). Mechanism captured by `SURGE_TRACE_EXEC=1` on a failing-window run: `ctrl_create=1`, `tasks_user=1`, `tasks_running=1`, `tasks_done=0`, `worker_sleep=32`, `wake_called=0`, `park_committed=0`, `reason=exit` — the entrypoint task is created and published, all 32 workers go to sleep without ever polling it, no wake is issued, and the process exits 0; `strace` confirms `exit_group(0)` with zero stdout writes. This is a startup lost-wake (task published ↔ worker sleep race), same hazard family as the PARKED-store protocol races, on the worker-pool bring-up path. Sync-main binaries are unaffected in the same windows. Matters for Epic 20: census/e2e rows on async programs can silently "pass" or spuriously fail; harnesses must assert an execution witness and may explicitly retry ONLY on the exact empty-output+exit-0 signature with a loud log naming this row. | Open | Runtime liveness follow-up (startup wake protocol); Epic 20 census tasks carry execution witnesses + labeled signature retries meanwhile | Minimal repro + root cause of the startup lost-wake; the exit path cannot report 0 while a created user task was never polled; census harness rows assert an execution witness; the labeled retries are removed. |
| RV2-DEBT-050 | An async `@entrypoint`'s return value does not become the process exit code: `@entrypoint async fn main() -> int { return 42; }` exits 0 on the LLVM/native backend (verified 2026-07-17; a SYNC `fn main() -> int` propagates its code correctly — the drop-reclamation e2e suite relies on it). The async entry path (task result → `__surge_start` → `rt_entry`) drops the result bits instead of converting them to the exit status. Consequence for harnesses: async e2e rows cannot signal failure through exit codes and must use stdout markers (execution witness + FAIL markers), which `TestRuntimeV2DropSelectSendArm` does. Found during Epic 20 Task 3 while wiring the select-send reclamation rows. | Open | Runtime entry-path follow-up (can ride any Epic 20 runtime task or a small standalone fix) | An async entrypoint returning `int` sets the process exit status like the sync form; a golden/e2e row covers both forms; async harness rows may then drop their stdout-marker workaround. |
| RV2-DEBT-051 | Crossing captures whose struct carries a HEAP field arrive corrupted and leak on the happy path (pre-existing — reproduced 2026-07-17 with the drop-registration changes stashed). Repro: `@shard_movable type Job = { id: int, note: string }` with `note: build_note(...)` (runtime-built string), `spawn on distributed { ret describe(own j); }`, `far Task.await()`. CLOSED 2026-07-17 by Epic 20 Task 5 row 2 — two independent defects, neither in the transport. (a) CORRUPTION = caller-side UAF before publish, not a pack/unpack mismatch: sema's statement-end temp-drop pass did not treat `own <expr>` as consuming its operand (`ExprUnaryOwn` had no `consumeTempCandidate`, unlike `&`/`&mut`), so the struct literal under `own Job{...}` stayed flagged and MIR dropped the "temp" at statement end — while BOTH backends lower `own` as identity over the boxed value, so the temp and the own binding are the SAME box: the drop freed the Job box `j` still pointed to, and the body read the freed box's slot 0 (allocator-reused pointer bits = the garbage `j.id`). All-Copy captures were masked because a no-heap-field struct is not droppable, so no statement-end drop was ever emitted for them. The UAF was NOT crossing-specific — any `own <producer>` of a heap-bearing struct dangled, local code included. FIX: `own` consumes its temp candidate (`internal/sema/type_expr_ops.go`; unit row `TestOwnOperandTransfersIntoBox`). The earlier "arity mismatch" evidence was a misread: the state box is 16B because `own T` layout resolves to T's layout (one slack slot), but pack, unpack, and registered drop all agree on one pointer field at offset 0. (b) LEAK = the shipped state envelope was never freed on the happy path: `__task_state()` hands the envelope to the body and nulls `task->state`; local async polls release their consumed state+payload boxes via `__async_state_free` before AsyncReturn (the resume-frame-reclamation model), but crossing poll bodies returned without the release. FIX: `rewriteSpawnOnPollReturns` releases the envelope box shallowly before AsyncReturn (captures were already unpacked at entry; the pending-side `__surge_drop_call` covers only never-handed-off states, so no state sees both releases) — covers spawn-on, immediate-on, and anchored bodies. PROOF: repro reads `j.id`/`j.note` correctly at shards 1/2/8; the crossing repro's valgrind definite-leak signature is now IDENTICAL to a no-crossing local-async control (shared baseline only); e2e `TestRuntimeV2CrossingHeapCaptureArrivesIntact` (mixed capture verified by content, shards 1/2/8, transport gate) + `TestRuntimeV2CrossingHeapCaptureCensusBalanced` (per-iteration far-loop heap growth == local spawn/await loop growth, 1 shard deterministic, drop-reclamation gate). Residual split to RV2-DEBT-052: every `compare` over an owned boxed union leaks the scrutinee box — local and far alike; the census row cancels it via the far-vs-local comparison and tightens automatically when 052 lands. | Closed (own consumes its operand; crossing bodies release the envelope; arrival + census e2e gated) | — | Done: a crossing body reads exactly the captured field values for mixed Copy/heap structs; the crossing adds zero happy-path heap growth over the local-await baseline; the Task 5 abandon rows build on the fixed contract. |
| RV2-DEBT-053 | A completed remote body's OWNED RESULT is never reclaimed when the awaiting caller abandons the reply (found 2026-07-19 by the Epic 20 Task 5 row-6 investigation, codex-verified then confirmed by direct read). The shipped-STATE contract (publication-accepted handoff) covers only the body's captures via `state_drop_fn_id`; the reply-edge RESULT has no drop obligation anywhere: (a) release-while-DONE — `dispatch_release` (`rt_remote_task_dispatch.c`) sees TASK_DONE, skips cancel, releases the owner task; `free_task` never inspects `task->result_bits`, so a heap-carried result (boxed bigint/string — any LLVM-boxed value) leaks permanently; (b) orphaned caller pending — a reply that already landed in the caller-side `rt_remote_task_pending.result_bits` is stranded forever if the caller task is cancelled before its next poll: the pending lives in an OPAQUE-POINTER async-frame slot with no drop tracking, and task teardown has no path that finds it — `rt_remote_task_pending.caller_task_id` is a DEAD FIELD for AWAIT/CANCEL/RELEASE ops (never assigned in `start_remote_task`/`rt_far_task_release`), while the EXECUTE/CHANNEL_SELECT ops set it and get exactly the needed sweep (`rt_immediate_on_release_owned`, op filter `rt_immediate_on.c` ~295). Plain-Copy results (fixnum) are inert bits — no manifestation. The leak is silent (no counter/log on the discard paths). Preferred fix per the investigation: (1) populate `caller_task_id` for AWAIT/CANCEL and widen the existing caller-teardown sweep's op filter (single-shot discipline mirroring `consume_handle` so it cannot race the compiled `finish_retry` consume), then (2) layer a `result_drop_fn_id` threaded like the state's `BodyFuncID` (payload type is known at the crossing lowering site) for the actual drop; a caller-side frame-drop sweep was considered and rejected as the same opaque-pointer-bypasses-ARC hazard class as RV2-DEBT-051. Stopgap candidate: a debug counter/assertion on the two discard paths to make the leak visible in census rows. **2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 5): the OWNER-SIDE half (053a) is CLOSED.** A `result_drop_fn_id` is now threaded from the spawn-on crossing (`emitSpawnOnCrossing`, keyed on the body's `PayloadType`) through `rt_remote_spawn_publish`/`_placement` and the spawn pending into the created body task (`task->result_drop_fn_id`), mirroring the state's `BodyFuncID`. `free_task` (`rt_task_lifetime.c`) drops an unconsumed heap result exactly once via a new `__surge_drop_result_call` dispatch + per-payload-type `drop_result.typeN` wrappers (its OWN id space keyed by TypeID, separate from the state switch's FuncID space, because a result may be a heap leaf — string/dyn-array — needing full value-drop, not just struct-box glue). Mutual exclusion with the consume path is by construction: every owner-side site that ships/hands out `result_bits` (the AWAIT reply in `dispatch_request` + `rt_remote_task_reply_owner_done`; local `rt_far_task_take_result`) clears `result_drop_fn_id` to 0 when ownership transfers, so a consumed result is never double-freed. The check lives in `free_task` (not a dispatch_release-only finalizer) because free_task is the sole free chokepoint for every abandon route and already hosts result-lease cleanup. Proof: deterministic `TestRuntimeV2RemoteTaskBehavior` rows `result-owner-release` (drop fires exactly once with the right id+pointer; negative control with the fix disabled fails "not dropped exactly once"), `result-copy-inert` (id 0 → no drop), `result-consumed-no-double-drop` (cleared → no double-free); LLVM emission row `TestEmitSpawnOnRegistersResultDropFn` pins the codegen. NOTE: a compiled-Surge heap-far-result leak is UNREACHABLE today — the non-copy reply gate (`crossingRecordExecutable`, Epic 21 Task 8, still closed) refuses non-copy far results at build time, so the fix is preventive machinery, dormant (resultDropID=0) for every currently-compilable program; the proofs therefore drive the owner-side free path directly (harness) and the codegen at MIR level (bypassing the gate). **2026-07-20 (Epic 21 Task 6): the CALLER-SIDE half (053b) is CLOSED.** `start_remote_task` now populates `caller_task_id` for AWAIT/CANCEL (previously a dead field on those two ops) and takes a `result_drop_fn_id`, threaded from the crossing lowering site's known payload type through `rt_far_task_await`/`rt_far_task_cancel`'s new parameter and stored on the pending. `rt_remote_task_pending`'s existing free path drops an unconsumed heap result exactly once via `__surge_drop_result_call`, mirroring the owner-side mechanism from 053a. A new caller-teardown sweep (`rt_remote_task_release_owned`, called from `mark_done` alongside the existing far-task and immediate-on sweeps) scans pendings by `caller_task_id` on task death and gives up the caller's own reference — deliberately not a routed cancel to the owner, since the owner-side `consume_handle` CAS was already spent by the original `.await()`/`.cancel()` and a second attempt would produce a bogus reply while leaking the owner's reference (confirmed by direct trace during design review, not assumed). The sweep must unlink-then-release (`rt_remote_task_pending_consume`, not a bare release) even though it may not be the last reference: a bare release without unlinking left the struct dangling in the registry list when it did turn out to be the last ref, caught by the negative-control harness rows before this closed (a real double-free, not hypothetical). Proof: five new `TestRuntimeV2RemoteTaskBehavior` rows — `caller-abandon-drops-landed-result-exactly-once`, `caller-abandon-copy-inert` and `caller-abandon-consumed-no-double-drop` (negative controls), `caller-abandon-filters-by-op-and-caller` (proves the sweep only touches the matching op+caller among siblings), `caller-abandon-in-flight-pending-survives` (proves a still-in-flight pending outlives the sweep on its remaining ref). Same UNREACHABLE note as 053a applies: the non-copy reply gate (Epic 21 Task 8, still open) keeps this dormant for every currently-compilable program today; the proofs drive the C-level pending lifecycle and MIR-level codegen directly. | Closed | Epic 20 Task 5 row 6 named the edge; 053a owner-side landed Epic 21 Task 5; 053b caller-side landed Epic 21 Task 6 | Both halves closed: a caller gone before consuming the reply reclaims a heap-carried result exactly once on both paths (release-while-DONE and orphaned landed reply); a census/harness row pins each; the AWAIT/CANCEL `caller_task_id` field is populated and load-bearing. |
| RV2-DEBT-052 | `compare` over an owned boxed union never frees the scrutinee box on the LLVM backend: the scrutinee moves into the lowering temp, arms extract the tag payload by copy, and nothing releases the union box afterwards — one 16-byte block per evaluated compare (e.g. every consumed `TaskResult` from `t.await()`), local and far alike. CLOSED 2026-07-19 (Epic 20 Task 8 row 1, codex-implemented, adversarially reviewed, lead-verified; fixed FIRST per the user's honest-strict-zero decision). Same class as RV2-DEBT-040: the `__cmpN` scrutinee is a synthetic normalize-pass let created after sema computed drop obligations. FIX: per-arm release synthesis in `normalizeCompareExpr` (`normalize_compare_release.go`) gated on ownership-transferring scrutinees (non-Copy incl. `@copy`-attr awareness, non-borrowed, union-kind): an arm whose payload positions are ALL plain bindings releases the box shallowly through the DEBT-040 channel (renamed to `StmtEnvelopeRelease`/`InstrEnvelopeRelease`, mnemonics `release_box`/`release_cursor` — it now serves both consumers honestly); no-payload/wildcard/fallback arms deep-drop through the union's drop glue; mixed payload shapes and the whole-value binding arm skip the release (leak over wrong-free, recorded residuals); guards release only in the passed branch. Sema soundness pinned both ways (`TestCompareOwnedScrutineeMarksBindingMoved` existing + new Copy-side counterpart). PROOF: valgrind on the spawn+await+compare control: 32B/2 blocks definitely lost -> 0/0 (also explains the two 16B blocks previously misattributed to entry states in the RV2-DEBT-051/053 investigations — they were TaskResult scrutinee boxes); 5-probe census `TestRuntimeV2CompareScrutineeReleaseCensusBalanced` at strict d(2000)-d(1)==0 (drop gate); the heap-capture census TIGHTENED from far==local equality to independent d8==d1 AND b8==b1. Found en route: RV2-DEBT-057 (union-cast box leak), RV2-DEBT-058 (compare-arm payload bindings never get scope-exit drops). | Closed (per-arm scrutinee release; strict-zero census; renamed shared envelope-release channel) | — | Done: a consumed union scrutinee frees exactly once on every arm shape that transfers ownership. RESIDUALS CLOSED 2026-07-31 (commit `80c74378`). The MIXED-payload arm leaked its envelope once per evaluation, measured at 384 bytes over 16 evaluations of `Pair(s, _) => len(s)`: a shallow free would have abandoned the ignored field and a deep drop would have freed the bound one twice, so neither ran. A position the pattern ignored now gets a binding of its own — the same `let` a named position gets, on the same arm-return drop list — which makes the arm all-bound and the existing shallow release correct again; a position that owns no heap needs no binding and stops blocking the release on its own account (the `int` in `Pair(s, _)` is bits the shallow free carries away). Only for a scrutinee the arm really owns: a borrowed one keeps its payload and a `@copy` one was cloned. A payload that CLONES when read is left alone too, and that one was learned the hard way: the first cut of this fix bound every ignored heap-owning position, which for a `@copy` composite gives the binding a CLONE while the original stays in the envelope — the arm then freed that envelope shallowly and abandoned it. Measured as a REGRESSION (16 bytes per evaluation of `compare Hold(Pair{...}) { Hold(_) => 1; ... }` with a `@copy` `Pair`, clean at the parent commit, where the arm was all-unbound and deep-dropped correctly); found by an independent review, fixed in `444efcc8`. The census could not see it because every union in it carried a `string`, which MOVES — it now also carries a non-`@copy` union holding a `@copy` payload, in the plain and the never-passing-guard spellings. Nested and literal payload patterns are STILL refused — they take out parts of a field, which the normalize pass does not model, and that is the remaining bound. The WHOLE-VALUE binding arm was re-measured and is already clean (0 definitely-lost over 16 evaluations), so it needed nothing. Gates: two new rows in `TestRuntimeV2CompareScrutineeReleaseCensusBalanced` at the same strict d(2000)-d(1)==0 — an arm binding the owning field while ignoring bits, and its mirror where the IGNORED field is the owner — red before the fix (exit 121). Also measured clean: two ignored fields at once, and a mixed arm behind a FAILING guard, on both backends. |
| RV2-DEBT-062 | The recursive composite drop-glue walk (`typeOwnsHeapRec`/`emitDropGlueBody`/`emitFieldDropAt`, `emit_drop_glue.go`) silently skips handle-shaped types — confirmed for `Task<T>` and `Channel<T>` — nested as a struct/tuple/union field or array element: `typeOwnsHeapRec`'s default case explicitly returns false for anything that isn't string/dynamic-array/struct/tuple/union (`emit_drop_glue.go` ~93-96, "Enums..., maps..., references, pointers, scalars: no owned heap the drop glue reclaims here"), so a field of one of these handle types is never visited, and the box it points to (a small runtime-owned wrapper — 24B for a local `Task<T>` handle observed) is never freed by the generic walk. Found 2026-07-20 while building RV2-DEBT-059's local repro: `TestRuntimeV2CancelledSuspendStateReclaimed`'s abandoned-state box packs a live `Task<T>` local (from `checkpoint().await()`) into its payload, and after 059's fix correctly frees the OUTER state box, exactly this residual remains (24B direct, git-stash A/B confirmed: 32B direct + 24B indirect before 059, 24B direct + 0 indirect after — the 32B outer box is the only thing 059's fix reclaims). A second, independent repro using `own Channel<int>` instead of `Task<T>` hit the SAME class at a different size (24B direct + 48B indirect, git-stash A/B: 32B+72B before, 24B+48B after) — confirms this isn't Task<T>-specific. These handle types are known to have their OWN dedicated reclaim mechanism elsewhere in the compiler (e.g. the far-channel case in `emitInstrDrop`, RV2-DEBT-060's closure note) for ordinary top-level scope-exit `InstrDrop`, but that mechanism is never consulted by the recursive composite walk when the same handle type shows up NESTED inside a larger value being dropped via `requireDropGlue`. This plausibly affects every OTHER existing consumer of `requireDropGlue`'s recursive descent too (crossing state via `registerCrossingDropState`, results via `registerCrossingDropResult`, and now RV2-DEBT-059's `registerAbandonedStateDrop`), not just the new mechanism that found it — any composite carrying a live Task<T>/Channel<T>/similar-handle field, dropped via any of these paths, would leak that field's own handle box. Not fixed here (out of scope for RV2-DEBT-059, which only needed the outer box). | Open | LLVM backend drop-glue generality (`emit_drop_glue.go`) — needs deciding how a nested handle-typed field routes to its OWN release call (not a raw `rt_free`) from inside generic recursive glue | `typeOwnsHeapRec`/`emitFieldDropAt` (or an equivalent dedicated path) recognizes handle-shaped types nested in a composite and routes each to its correct release call; the two known repros (`TestRuntimeV2CancelledSuspendStateReclaimed`'s Task<T> case, and the sibling Channel<T> case referenced in its comment) both go to strict zero; any other `requireDropGlue` consumer carrying a live handle-typed field is audited for the same gap. |
| RV2-DEBT-063 | `run_ready_one` (`runtime/native/rt_async_poll.c`, the single-worker synchronous poll path reached via `rt_task_await`/`run_until_done` whenever `rt_worker_count() <= 1`) has its own inline duplicate `poll_outcome` switch (two copies in the same function) that, on `POLL_DONE_CANCELLED`, calls `mark_done()` directly — it never routes through `apply_poll_outcome()`, unlike every other poller in the codebase (`rt_worker_turn.c`'s three call sites, `poll_ready_child_inline` in `rt_async_task.c`). `apply_poll_outcome`'s `POLL_DONE_CANCELLED` case is where a cancelled task with its OWN scope still holding active children gets deferred (re-parked on `scope_key`, `task->cancel_pending = 1`) instead of completing immediately — structured concurrency's guarantee that a parent never finishes before its children do. `run_ready_one` skips that deferral entirely: in single-worker mode (`SURGE_SHARDS=1`/no extra threads, the synchronous `.await()` path), a task cancelled while its own scope has active children completes immediately regardless, which is not just a leak variant of RV2-DEBT-059/062 but a correctness gap — a parent can be freed/observed-done while its children are still running.
